Forward-looking statements

Throughout this report, there are certain statements made that are "forward-looking statements". Any statements
preceded or followed by, or that include the words "forecasts", "believes", "expects", "intends", "plans",
"predictions", "will", "may", "should", "could", "anticipates", "estimates", "seeks", "continues", or similar
expressions or the negative thereof, are forward-looking statements.

By their nature, forward-looking statements are speculative and allude to known and unknown risks, opportunities,
macro-economic issues and any factors that could cause the actual results, performance or achievements of the Group
to be materially different from the future results, performance or achievements expressed or implied by such forward-
looking statements.

Forward-looking statements are not guarantees of future performance and reflect the Group's view at the date of
publication of this report.

The Group is not obliged to publicly update or revise these forward-looking statements for events or circumstances
occurring after the date of publication of this report. Any forward-looking statement contained herein based on current
trends and/or activities of the Group should not be taken as a representation that such trends or activities will continue
in the future. No statement in this document is intended to be a profit forecast or to imply that the earnings of
the Group for the current year or future years will necessarily match or exceed the historical or published earnings of
the Group.

Forward-looking statements should not be relied on because they involve uncertainties and known and unknown risks,
which risk factors are described throughout the commentary in this report, and include economic, business and political
conditions in South Africa and elsewhere.

Assurance                                                                                                                                                                                           Omnia Holdings Limited

Overseen by a working group led by the FD, the integrated reporting process begins with a review of the
previous year's report to identify opportunities for improved disclosure and to ensure continued alignment with
best practice in reporting. In addition, this process ensures that the content of our Integrated Report aligns with
our strategic objectives, and complies with relevant regulatory requirements and various standards which we
apply.

In terms of a structured internal and external reporting review programme, the integrity of the information in this 3
Integrated Report is assured both internally and externally.

· Internally, the relevant governance structures and processes are in place. Executive management monitors and
   reviews the document while at board level, the audit and risk committee (ARC) oversees the Group-wide internal
   control environment, and thus the integrity of information, and approves the information published in external reports.
   From a governance perspective, our risk, audit and compliance functions and related assurance activities are aligned
   so as to support the integrity of the information presented.

· Externally, the Annual Financial Statements were audited by Deloitte & Touche who provided an unqualified audit for
   the 2026 reports - see our Annual Financial Statements FY26 for their audit opinion - while SLR provided assurance
   in accordance with the AA1000 Assurance standard version three at a moderate assurance level on selected
   sustainability information. See our ESG Report 2026 for their assurance statement.

About Omnia

The Omnia Group is a global, diversified chemicals company supplying chemicals and specialised services and solutions to the
agriculture, mining and chemicals application industries. The Group combines technical innovation and intellectual capital to add value
for customers at every stage of the supply and service chain.

Omnia is committed to shaping a brighter and more promising future for all stakeholders by sustaining livelihoods, providing growth
opportunities and using technology to ensure the efficient use of resources.

                                                                                               Our purpose

                                                     Innovating to enhance life, together creating a greener future.

                                                                         Our purpose has four supporting pillars aimed at:

             Enhancing lives                         Sustaining livelihoods       Optimising the use of                          Caring for our planet
                                                                                     natural resources

Our investment case

Omnia has consistently demonstrated its ability to create sustainable long-term value for shareholders. Our attractive customer
proposition and track record for operational excellence have positioned the Company for continued future growth.

Our compelling investor proposition is based on the following:

                                                               We create sustained long-term value for all
                                                               stakeholders by optimising and balancing the use of
                                                               scarce resource inputs and relationships in the
                                                               conduct of our business.

    How we create value

    Omnia's strategy is aimed at sustainably growing the
    business for a safer, greener future. The objective is to
    protect and grow our business by building on existing
    strengths, creating further value from the core
    business, and sustainably allocating capital resources
    to position the business for global growth. Our
    strategy ensures that we lead with purpose as we
    innovate to enhance life, together creating a greener
    future. ESG principles are core to the strategy.

Our strategy

Omnia's strategy is to sustainably grow the business by building on existing strengths, maximising value from our core business and
maintaining disciplined capital allocation. By delivering on our strategy, we will enable Omnia to expand globally and deliver on its purpose.

Our strategy

                                                         Omnia's strategy is to grow and diversify our business geographically

                                           Our strategy, which is focused on growth, diversification, resilience and agility, is based on these three strategic pillars:

Business model

Our business model describes how we optimise and balance the use of scarce resource inputs and
relationships in the conduct of our business to ensure delivery on our strategy and its objectives,
and in so doing creating sustained long-term value for all stakeholders.

               Governance framework

                                External                                                             In addition to our strategy and the management of the necessary inputs, our business model
                               operating                                                             takes into account the external operating environment, stakeholders and their needs and
                             environment                                                             expectations, and the resulting risks, opportunities and material issues.

                               Strategy:                                                             External drivers of our business include prevailing economic and geopolitical conditions,
               How we allocate and manage the                                                        agricultural and mining output, weather patterns and commodity prices.

                    sustainable use of scarce                                                        Guiding all that we do is our governance framework, our values and code of ethics.

Managing capital trade-offs for value creation

Together our business model and strategy guide our business decisions and actions, and the disciplined allocation and use of our
capital resources.

Top risks

      Country risk and regional instability                                                                             Safety performance

Management of geopolitical, regulatory and localisation complexity across the business'                            Safety management to prevent injuries and fatalities, environmental damage, regulatory
geographic footprint to support operational consistency, compliance and value realisation.                         sanctions, reputational harm and financial loss.

What this risk means                                                                                               What this risk means
The business is exposed to legislative, tax and localisation risks in key jurisdictions and these may result in    The inherent nature of our operations involves hazardous materials and physical assets. Failure to maintain a
regulatory breaches, penalties, operational disruptions, execution complexity, higher compliance costs,            strong safety culture, workforce capability and rigorous standards could lead to incidents causing harm to
reputational damage and reduced ability to operate effectively in that market.                                     people, the environment or physical assets, resulting in severe regulatory, commercial and reputational
                                                                                                                   consequences.

Key drivers of the risk                                                                                            Key drivers of the risk

· Wide geographic footprint increases exposure to diverse cultures, laws and operating practices                   · Inherent health and safety risks associated with operations
· Changes in tax legislation and increased scrutiny by tax authorities, tax policy and cross-border tax treatment  · Consistency and alignment of safety culture, workforce capability, processes and standards across multiple
· Multi-jurisdictional compliance requirements, including labour, tax and foreign exchange
· Geopolitical instability (conflicts, sanctions, tariffs and trade embargoes) affecting supply chain and market      jurisdictions
                                                                                                                   · Applicable regulatory and licensing requirements across multiple jurisdictions
   access
· Reliance/dependency on local partners and third parties for safety, compliance and market access                                                                                                                                Integrated Annual Report 2026

   tracking and structured escalation of emerging risks                                                               structures and ongoing compliance monitoring

· Ongoing monitoring of regulatory, tax and foreign exchange requirements using specialists and in-country         · Regular site-level inspections, audits and incident reporting processes with corrective action tracking and
   governance structures                                                                                              regulatory alignment

· Use of regional legal, tax and geopolitical specialists to support compliance and navigation of complex          · Mandatory safety training programmes and contractor SHEQ onboarding to ensure consistent safety
   operating environments                                                                                             standards

· Multi-sourcing, flexible formulations and strategic stockholding to mitigate supply disruption, currency         · Engineering controls, preventative maintenance and statutory inspections for high-risk plant and process
   volatility and cross-border constraints                                                                            environments

· Geographic diversification across markets and commodities to reduce concentration risk and exposure to           · Reinforcement of a safety-first culture through leadership visibility, behavioural safety initiatives and
   single-country instability                                                                                         continuous improvement programmes

Managing risks and opportunities continued

      Supply chain and third-party risk (Previously third-party risk)                                                      Management of our talent

Supply chain and third-party ecosystem management to support operational continuity and                              Attraction, development and retention of critical skills to support operational capacity, execution
mitigate regulatory, safety, fraud, reputational and financial risks.                                                capability and long-term sustainability.

What this risk means                                                                                                 What this risk means
Dependence on key suppliers, contractors, logistics partners and other third parties exposes the Group to            Challenges in securing and retaining critical talent affects the Group's ability to execute strategy, maintain
operational, regulatory and reputational risks where these parties fail to meet performance, compliance or           operational performance and sustain leadership continuity, with consequences for productivity, resilience and
service requirements. Such failures may disrupt production, product availability and service delivery, resulting in  long-term growth.
increased costs, operational inefficiencies and impacts on business performance.

Key drivers of the risk                                                                                              Key drivers of the risk                                                                                         Integrated Annual Report 2026
· Dependency on key suppliers (raw material, logistics partners, contractors and service providers) across           · Competitive labour markets and challenging operating environments
                                                                                                                     · Long recruitment lead times, particularly for senior and specialist roles
   multiple jurisdictions                                                                                            · Skills shortages and reliance on key personnel
· Deviation from contractual, safety, environmental or performance requirements by third parties
· Risk of regulatory breaches, safety incidents and environmental non-compliance                                     Mitigating measures
· Procurement irregularities (fraud, collusion and corruption) and conflicts of interest                             · Implementation of a structured talent management and workforce planning framework aligned to business

Mitigating measures                                                                                                     strategy and critical skills requirements
· Comprehensive third-party onboarding and vetting processes, including legal, financial, regulatory and SHEQ        · Competitive remuneration structures and targeted retention initiatives for scarce and critical roles
                                                                                                                     · Succession planning, leadership development and internal talent mobility programmes to reduce key-person
   due diligence
· Implementation of standardised supplier contracts and service level agreements incorporating compliance,              dependency
                                                                                                                     · Targeted training and development programmes, including technical upskilling and leadership capability
   ethics and audit rights
· Ongoing supplier performance monitoring, audits and periodic reassessment of risk exposure                            building
· Supplier and logistics diversification strategies to reduce dependency on single sources and mitigate              · Graduate programmes and university partnerships to develop a sustainable pipeline of future talent

Managing risks and opportunities continued

      Technology continuity and resilience

Secure, reliable and resilient information technology management to support operational
continuity and mitigate cyber and business continuity risks.

What this risk means
System outages, cyber incidents can interrupt operations, compromise data and reduce confidence in the
resilience of the information technology environment.

Key drivers of the risk

· Continued cyber attacks
· Threats arising from AI and rapid technological change
· Unsupported legacy systems

Mitigating measures                                                                                                                                                                                      Integrated Annual Report 2026

· Implementation of IT governance structures and formal oversight forums to monitor risk and control
   effectiveness

· Ongoing cybersecurity management, including penetration testing, Security Operations Centre monitoring
   and data protection controls

· Formal incident response and disaster recovery frameworks with defined escalation protocols
· Backup, replication and system redundancy processes to ensure availability and recovery capability
· Third-party IT service provider oversight to ensure resilience and service delivery standards
· AI governance framework

the long-term sustainability of our business. Strong governance provides the                                          supports our operating model, underpins the renewal of our
foundation that enables us to deliver on our global diversification aspirations and                                   culture and has revitalised our stakeholder engagement. Our
significant growth opportunities.                                                                                     governance framework ensures that our business and ESG

                                                                                                                      excellence.

FD's report continued

The continued strength of the Group's balance sheet and liquidity
position, as affirmed by Omnia's long-term and short-term credit
ratings being maintained at A+ and A1 respectively, with a stable
outlook, underscores confidence in the Group's financial resilience
and its ability to deliver sustainable long-term value.

Acknowledgements
In closing, I would like to extend my sincere appreciation to the entire
Omnia team for their continued dedication in delivering strong results
in a complex environment. Your commitment to "living our purpose
and shaping our future", excellence, innovation and collaboration
remains central to our success.

I also wish to thank our board, exco and shareholders for their
ongoing support and guidance as we build on this momentum to
deliver sustainable growth and long-term value creation.

Stephan Serfontein

ESG performance - a summary

Our ESG Report 2026 provides a comprehensive description of our sustainability and ESG                           Climate action, decarbonisation and resilience                                                                 Integrated Annual Report 2026
approach, our targets, goals and commitments, our performance during the year and our
expectations and plans for the year ahead.                                                                       Climate change remains one of the most significant sustainability challenges facing our business, our
                                                                                                                 customers and broader society.
While sustainability is embedded in everything that we do, we recognise that some stakeholders require
more detailed and specific information, and hence the publication of this report at the same time as we          As a supplier to the agriculture and mining sectors, we recognise that our operations and value chains both
publish our Integrated Annual Report. The following information is an extract from our ESG Report. The full      contribute to and are affected by climate-related impacts. At the same time, we believe our products,
report may be found online at https://omnia.co.za/media/esg-report-2026.pdf.                                     technologies and expertise can play an important role in supporting more sustainable agricultural
                                                                                                                 production, improving resource efficiency and enabling safer and more responsible mining practices. As a
Our approach                                                                                                     result, climate action is increasingly integrated into our strategy, governance, operational planning and
                                                                                                                 investment decisions.
Sustainability is integral to our purpose of innovating to enhance life, together creating a greener future. It
shapes how we create value, manage risk, allocate capital and position our business for long-term success        Our climate ambition extends beyond reducing our own environmental footprint. We aim to build a resilient
in a rapidly changing world.                                                                                     business capable of responding to physical climate risks, while supporting customers in adapting to
                                                                                                                 changing climatic conditions and transitioning to lower-carbon operating models. We view decarbonisation
During FY26, we continued to embed ESG considerations across our operations, products, services and              as both an environmental imperative and a strategic opportunity: one that can strengthen energy security,
value chains, recognising that responsible business practices are essential to maintaining competitiveness,      improve operational efficiency, reduce costs, increase resilience and support innovation throughout our
strengthening resilience and delivering sustainable returns for our stakeholders. Our approach reflects a        value chain.
conviction that commercial success and sustainable development are mutually reinforcing, and that our
ability to create value over the long term depends on how effectively we respond to the environmental and        During FY26, climate considerations became more deeply embedded in our governance, risk management
social challenges facing the industries we serve.                                                                and investment decision-making processes. Climate-related performance indicators continued to be
                                                                                                                 incorporated into management scorecards, while oversight of climate-related risks and opportunities
The year represented an important step in the implementation of our ESG strategy, broadening our focus           remained a standing focus area for the SEC and executive leadership. This reflects our view that climate
beyond manufacturing operations to encompass the full lifecycle of our products and services.                    change is not solely an environmental issue, but a strategic business issue with implications for operational
                                                                                                                 resilience, cost competitiveness, energy security and long-term growth.
The strategy was developed through an extensive review of our impacts, risks and opportunities, and was
informed by stakeholder engagement, emerging regulatory requirements and evolving sustainability                 We continued to make meaningful progress against our climate objectives. Scope 1 and Scope 2
expectations. It continues to provide a framework for integrating sustainability into strategic decision making  greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions declined to 188 172 tCOe, 16% down on our FY24 baseline, while GHG
while enabling us to identify opportunities for innovation, operational improvement and value creation.          intensity improved to 0.047 tCOe per tonne manufactured. Net energy efficiency improved to 0.25 GJ per
                                                                                                                 tonne manufactured, exceeding the trajectory required to achieve our FY30 target.
Our ESG framework is structured around four pillars - shaping the future, protecting the planet, enhancing
lives and securing trust - which together provide a holistic approach to managing our most material              These improvements were achieved by a combination of improved operational efficiencies, process
sustainability matters and creating long-term value.                                                             optimisation, reduced reliance on purchased steam and increased renewable energy generation.
                                                                                                                 Importantly, we remain on track to achieve our FY30 target to reduce Scope 1 and Scope 2 emissions.
Through our double materiality assessment, we strengthened our understanding of the environmental and
social impacts associated with our operations and products, as well as the ways in which external
sustainability challenges may affect our business, financial performance and prospects. This process
enabled us to prioritise the issues most critical to our long-term success and reinforced the importance of
integrating sustainability considerations into our governance structures, risk management processes and
growth ambitions.

Corporate governance

As a responsible corporate citizen, Omnia recognises that sound governance is essential to sustainable value creation. As our strategic journey has progressed from stabilising and
fixing to protecting and growing our core business and expanding our international presence, governance has remained a critical enabler of our strategic ambitions. Through the
embedding of robust governance practices, supported by comprehensive policies and procedures, we continue to strengthen our culture, optimise processes and unlock
organisational synergies, ensuring our business evolves in a responsible and sustainable manner.

Strong governance provides the foundation to deliver on our global diversification aspirations and                The following were also implemented to ensure that ethical standards are maintained at an operational        Integrated Annual Report 2026
significant growth opportunities.                                                                                 level to foster a culture of integrity and accountability within the Group:
                                                                                                                  · Established clear policies: Comprehensive ethical guidelines and codes of conduct that outline
Having solid governance processes and policies in place supports our new operating model and underpins
our culture and engagement with employees and other stakeholders. As we enter the next phase of our                  expected behaviours and standards have been developed and implemented. These documents are
strategy, it continues to ensure alignment with our ESG priorities, while instilling a culture of excellence         accessible and understandable to all employees
and performance.
                                                                                                                  · Leadership commitment: Leaders model ethical behaviour and demonstrate a commitment to our
Ethical leadership                                                                                                   ethical standards. This sets the tone for the entire organisation and influences the behaviour of all
                                                                                                                     employees
The board is ultimately responsible for the ethical conduct of the business and considers sound corporate
governance and an ethical culture as critical to sustainability and this remains a priority focus for the board.  · Training and education: Regular training sessions and workshops on ethical issues relevant to Omnia's
We are committed to sustaining best practice in terms of standards of ethics, transparency and good                  activities are conducted. This reinforces the importance of an ethical culture and keeps employees
governance and to following strict compliance procedures.                                                            informed about best practice and emerging ethical concerns

that ethics are effectively handled.                                                                                 established with an anonymous whistle-blowing hotline that is overseen by Deloitte. Direct reporting
                                                                                                                     mechanisms are also encouraged. This ensures that there is no fear of retaliation for the reporting of

employees, customers, suppliers, financial institutions and communities. In doing so, we advocate King
IV's ethical and effective governance outcomes: an ethical culture, good performance, effective control           · Ethics audits and reviews: Periodical audits and reviews to assess adherence to ethical standards are
and legitimacy. The board further endorses the principles of integrity, competence, responsibility,                  conducted. These help to identify potential issues early and allow for corrective action to be taken

Board focus areas FY27

· Oversee alignment of governance structures, processes and outcomes with the principles of King V
· Strengthen board diversity by appointing suitably qualified and experienced individuals to replace resigning members, ensuring alignment with the required skills, experience and strategic priorities of the organisation
· Ensure management maintains disciplined capital allocation, balancing growth investments, partnerships and shareholder returns

                                                                                                                 Future focus areas

                                                                                                                 Looking ahead, the committee will continue to assess the mix of fixed to variable pay for executives
                                                                                                                 to ensure that it remains competitive, sustainable and aligned to the market, and will review the
                                                                                                                 incentive plans to ensure they remain effective, affordable and appropriate in supporting the Group's 83
                                                                                                                 current performance trajectory.

                                                                                                                 A key focus will be to strengthen the frameworks and processes that underpin meaningful analysis of
                                                                                                                 our pay practices to ensure that remuneration remains fair and free from discrimination at all levels in
                                                                                                                 the organisation, enhancing transparency and facilitating an approach to address the pay gap in a
                                                                                                                 sustainable manner.

                                                                                                                          Who is included in the disclosures
Stephan Serfontein                 88                                                                                     All South African employees, both permanent and temporary, totalling 2 544, were included in the review,
                                                                     200                                                  except our 19 apprentices and six learners.

                 Tiaan Kotzé                           168                                                                Apprentices were excluded from the calculations due to their non-standard, developmental employment                                 Omnia Holdings Limited
98                                                               200                                                      terms which would distort the ratios, detracting from the purpose of the disclosures. On completion of
                                                                                                                          their three-year training programmes, this group is absorbed into the business and their pay and
             Ralf Hennecke         100                                                                                    employment terms normalised for the roles they fill. Similarly, learners, who receive a stipend from the
                                                                 200                                                      Company while they complete their studies, are excluded from the calculations.

                              l Progress Mar 2026 l Target level Mar 2030                                                 Individuals employed by foreign subsidiaries would only be included in the disclosures if they were
                                                                                                                          executive directors or prescribed officers as foreign pay practices and exchange rate conversions impair
                                                                                                                          comparability and including employees of foreign entities would exceed the purpose of the amendments
                                                                                                                          to the Companies Act, which is principally aimed at addressing income inequality within South Africa.

                                                                                                                          Currently, all executives are employed in South Africa and were included in the disclosures.

                                                                                                                          How do we calculate total remuneration
                                                                                                                          For purposes of the review and calculations, earnings were disclosed on a payroll basis for consistency
                                                                                                                          across the employee base and included all amounts paid to the employees during FY26, including salaries,
                                                                                                                          benefits, variable pay, long service awards and severance payments. The amounts differ from the FY26
                                                                                                                          tables of executive directors' and prescribed officers' remuneration due to the STI and LTI values. The
                                                                                                                          executive remuneration tables include the STI receivable for FY26 performance, payable through payroll in 98
                                                                                                                          July 2026, whereas the wage gap disclosures include the STI paid through payroll during FY26, based on
                                                                                                                          FY25 performance. When LTI shares are committed to MSR, there is no cash settlement value processed
                                                                                                                          through payroll, although the illustrative vesting value is included in the executive remuneration
                                                                                                                          disclosures.

                                                                                                                          Earnings included dividends received by employees on unvested shares.

                                                                                                                          The earnings of employees that joined during the reporting period were annualised for a more meaningful
                                                                                                                          assessment of internal pay equity across the workforce.

                                                                                                                          Being the first year of implementation, the current year disclosures create a base against which to track
                                                                                                                          year-on-year movements. Disclosures will be extended in future reporting periods.

                          Recordable case rate is a safety indicator - a recordable case in any incident
                          resulting in occupational illness and/or injury which arises out of or during an                                                                                                            103
                          employee's normal course of duty and the execution of work-related
                          responsibilities and which, as a result, requires medical treatment excluding                                                                                                               Omnia Holdings Limited
                          diagnostics. Medical treatment is defined as treatment above and beyond first
                          aid, administered by a recognised professional medical practitioner such as a
                          medical doctor, paramedic or nurse. RCR is therefore the number of recordable
                          cases per year or 200 000 working/exposure hours
